# Kushida Kikyō

*Character · Main · 1-D*

Kushida Kikyō is named in 33 of 36 volumes. 163 scenes from 28 volumes have been read line by line, with a page for every line.

- **Appears in:** Year 1 · Year 2 · Year 3

## Scenes

### Year 1, Volume 1

#### p.19–24 — Entrance ceremony bus: the public champion

Crowded city bus heading to ANHS on entrance ceremony morning. An elderly woman is standing in the aisle, unsteady on her feet. An office lady scolds Kōenji first; most passengers look away or pretend not to notice.

- **What happens** (p.21) — An unnamed girl wearing the ANHS uniform — the text never names her here, but she is the one later understood to be Kushida (see [note]) — steps forward from the aisle and directly faces Kōenji, who is lounging in the priority seating.
- **Spoken** (p.21) — “Um… I think that the lady is right.” (unexpected support, delivered brave and frank)
- **Spoken** (p.21) — “This poor woman appears to have been suffering for quite some time now. Won’t you offer up your seat? While you might consider such courtesy unnecessary, I think it would contribute greatly to society.”
- **What happens** (p.21–22) — When Kōenji haughtily refuses and declares he cares only for his own satisfaction, she refuses to back down or accept defeat. Instead of retreating, she turns to face the entire crowded bus without fear.
- **Spoken** (p.22) — “Everyone, please listen to me for just a moment. Won’t someone give up their seat for this woman? It doesn’t matter who. Please.”
- **Feat** (p.22–24) — Extraordinary social courage and charisma; her appeal moves an office lady to stand and surrender her seat out of guilt (“E-excuse me. You can have mine.”), settling the matter.
- **Voice** (p.22) — Earnest, pleading, gentle, highly moral public register that commands collective attention without sounding aggressive.
- **Relationship · Kōenji Rokusuke** (p.21) — Direct moral clash; attempts to appeal to social conscience against his absolute solipsism.
- **Caveat** (p.19–24) — The bus text never names the uniformed challenger. Her identification with Kushida is an inference (she later knows AK and Horikita spoke at the gate that day, p.57), not a stated fact in Year 1, Vol. 1.

#### p.34–35 — Classroom Day 1: empathetic rescue & friendship declaration

Class 1-D classroom after homeroom orientation. Hirata asks students to introduce themselves from the front row.

- **Post** (p.35) — Established as the idol and social anchor of Class 1-D.
- **What happens** (p.34) — Inogashira Kokoro freezes at her desk, trembling and pale. Classmates mistakenly shout “Do your best!” which heightens her panic. Kushida steps in with immediate, emotionally intelligent guidance.
- **Spoken** (p.34) — “It’s okay to go slowly. Don’t rush.”
- **Feat** (p.34) — Instinctive social psychology: understands that “Do your best!” applies forceful pressure, whereas “Just take things slowly. Don’t rush,” removes urgency and restores calm. Inogashira successfully finishes her intro.
- **What happens** (p.34–35) — Steps up cheerfully as the next speaker, glowing with approachable energy.
- **Spoken** (p.35) — “My name is Kushida Kikyou. None of my friends from junior high made it to this school, so I’m alone here. I’d like to get to know all of your names and faces right away and become friends as soon as possible!”
- **Spoken** (p.35) — “My first goal is to become friends with everyone. So, after we’re finished with introductions, I’d love for you to share your contact information with me!”
- **Spoken** (p.35) — “So, after school or during vacations, I want to make all sorts of memories with lots of people. Please feel free to invite me to lots and lots of events! Anyway, I’ve talked for a long time, so I’ll end my introduction here.”
- **Trait** (p.35) — Outward persona: boundless warmth, radical accessibility, open-hearted desire to unite the entire class and befriend every student without distinction.
- **Position** (p.35) — Instantly established as the undisputed idol and social anchor of Class 1-D.

#### p.56–57 — Corridor interception: probing for Horikita's secrets

On Ayanokōji’s way out of the classroom at lunch on Day 2 (he’d resolved to eat alone). Kushida approaches him with a radiant smile, holding something he can’t identify — possibly a pouch covered in key holders.

- **What happens** (p.56) — Calls out to him cheerfully (“You’re Ayanokouji-kun, right?”); Ayanokōji’s heart pounds like a jackhammer at her beauty (short, straight dyed-brown hair, one of the newly approved short skirt lengths).
- **Spoken** (p.56) — “I’m Kushida, from your class. Do you remember me?” she asked.
- **Spoken** (p.56) — “To tell you the truth, there’s something I wanted to ask you. It’s just one little question. Ayanokouji-kun, are you on good terms with Horikita-san?”
- **Spoken** (p.56) — “Oh, no. Well, do you remember when I said I wanted to get along with everyone in class? That’s why I wanted everyone’s contact info. But… Horikita turned me down.”
- **What happens** (p.57) — When he says he barely knows Horikita, she pivots to probing what kind of person she is, then frames her interest as pointed “worry.”
- **Spoken** (p.57) — “I was just wondering what kind of person Horikita-san is,” (Kushida continued)
- **Spoken** (p.57) — “Is she the type who’ll talk a lot when she’s with a friend?”
- **Spoken** (p.57) — “Well, during our introductions, Horikita-san walked out of the classroom, right? It seems like she hasn’t talked to anybody yet, so I’m a little worried about her.”
- **Spoken** (p.57) — “Hmm. I see. I thought that you two must have been old friends before starting school here. I’m sorry to have asked you such a strange question.”
- **Spoken** (p.57) — “How? You introduced yourself the other day, didn’t you? I remembered.” (she’d listened to his hopelessly lame self-introduction)
- **What happens** (p.57) — Closes with a radiant handshake: “Well, it’s nice to meet you again, Ayanokouji-kun.”
- **Trait** (p.56–57) — Relentless social campaigner; frames investigative probing as friendly concern; remembers even the least remarkable classmates’ introductions; warmth weaponized as an information-gathering tool.
- **Voice** (p.56–57) — Bubbly, innocent, persistently encouraging, masking an unyielding determination.
- **Relationship · Horikita Suzune** (p.56–57) — Fixation begins: determined to break through Horikita’s barriers; encodes her as the girl who refused contact info and “hasn’t talked to anybody yet.”
- **Relationship · Ayanokōji Kiyotaka** (p.56–57) — Recruits him as her bridge and information source to reach Horikita; establishes their first warm real-world contact (handshake).

#### p.79, 87–88 — Swim heat & detecting Ayanokōji's physique

Indoor swimming pool.

- **Feat** (p.79) — Races in the second girls' swimming heat; claims she wasn't good at swimming in junior high, yet finishes second with a very respectable time of **approximately 31 seconds**.
- **What happens** (p.87–88) — Stands beside Ayanokōji by the pool deck in her school swimsuit; watches Kōenji and Sudō with genuine enthusiasm.
- **Spoken** (p.87) — “I’m really looking forward to this. Both Kouejin-kun and Sudou-kun are so fast,” (corpus renders Kōenji’s name as “Kouejin-kun” here — kept verbatim)
- **Spoken** (p.87) — “Hmm? What’s the matter? Your face looks red for some reason. Are you not feeling well, by chance?”
- **Spoken** (p.88) — “You’re much faster than average, too, Ayanokouji-kun.”
- **What happens** (p.88) — Observes Ayanokōji's bare upper body with keen perception, looking at his muscular build with shock and awe.
- **Spoken** (p.88) — “Is that so? But you look like a really manly guy, Ayanokouji-kun. Even though you’re so slim, I could say that you’re even better built than Sudou, and he plays basketball.”
- **Feat** (p.88) — Perceptive observation: first classmate to pierce Ayanokōji's unassuming exterior and physically recognize his elite, hidden muscle development despite his deflections about "good genes".
- **Relationship · Ayanokōji Kiyotaka** (p.87–88) — Casual, warm banter; notices his flushed nervousness and compliments his physique.

#### p.89, 91–93, 98–101 — Café trap failure & the compulsion for 100% friendship

Classroom 1-D; outside dorms; then Palate Café in Keyaki Mall. Palate Café. Kushida walks in, acting surprised, but Horikita immediately exposes the four-person table and nervous glances as a transparent setup.

- **What happens** (p.89) — Smilingly invites Horikita to a café after class; when told not to invite her again because it's a bother, Kushida maintains her bright grin and promises: *“I’ll invite you again.”*
- **What happens** (p.91) — Waits outside the dormitory for Ayanokōji. Takes his hand warmly, stepping into his personal space with a floral scent.
- **Spoken** (p.91) — “I’ll just ask you outright. Ayanokouji-kun, have you seen Horikita-san smile even once?”
- **What happens** (p.91–93) — Conspires with Ayanokōji to stage an ambush meeting disguised as coincidence at Palate Café in Keyaki Mall, directing him to lure Horikita there under the pretense of treating her to tea.
- **What happens** (p.99) — Faced with Horikita's icy refusal to sit, Kushida mumbles an ominous hint of prior recognition.
- **Spoken** (p.99) — “Horikita-san, whatever you say, I really want to be friends with you. When I saw you, I felt like it wasn’t the first time we’d met. I wondered if you felt the same way.”
- **What happens** (p.99–100) — Horikita cuts her off, drops her bombshell about being alone for nine consecutive years, and leaves.
- **What happens** (p.100) — Kushida collapses silently into her seat, genuinely stunned and winded by the brutal rebuff.
- **What happens** (p.100) — Recovers her trademark sunshine smile in a split-second.
- **Spoken** (p.100) — “It’s okay. Thank you, Ayanokouji-kun. It’s true that I wasn’t able to become her friend, but…I was able to learn something important. That’s enough for me.”
- **What happens** (p.101) — Moves to sit beside Ayanokōji at the table. When asked why she is so desperate to befriend Horikita when she already has more friends than anyone else, Kushida reveals her underlying psychological compulsion.
- **Spoken** (p.101) — “I wanted to be friends with everyone. Not just the people in Class D, but the students from other classes, too. But if I can’t become friends with one girl in my class, then that means I’ll never achieve my goal…”
- **Trait** (p.100–101) — Terrifying emotional switch: can collapse in despair and snap back to a beaming smile in an instant; driven by an absolute, obsessive psychological need for 100% universal social acceptance—a single holdout (Horikita) invalidates her entire self-worth.
- **Relationship · Horikita Suzune** (p.99) — Suspects or hints at a prior encounter from junior high; fixated on conquering her wall.
- **Relationship · Ayanokōji Kiyotaka** (p.91, 100–101) — Deepens alliance; relies on him as a co-conspirator; surprised to learn he was also friendless in junior high.

#### p.117–118 — Group outing & grounding Yamauchi

Keyaki Mall boutique and café.

- **What happens** (p.117) — Participates in the class shopping outing with Hirata, Karuizawa, Ike, Yamauchi, and Ayanokōji.
- **Spoken** (p.117) — “Ha ha! I get the feeling Ike-kun is enjoying his time here to the fullest!”
- **Spoken** (p.117) — “Do you mean you’ll lose your sense of money management? Yeah, that does sound pretty scary, actually.” (concurs with Hirata that unearned wealth is dangerous).
- **Spoken** (p.118) — “What about you, Ayanokouji-kun? Do you think that 100,000 points is a lot or not enough?”
- **What happens** (p.118) — When Yamauchi absurdly boasts that he scored 900 points on the "APEC", Kushida gently and politely brings him back to reality without embarrassing him.
- **Spoken** (p.118) — “Um, don’t you mean the TOEIC, not APEC?”
- **Trait** (p.117–118) — Socially unifying, attentive to everyone in the conversation, checks in on quiet members (Ayanokōji), defuses male boasting with gentle charm.

#### p.131–132, 134, 138–139 — De-escalating violence & lending points without hesitation

Classroom 1-D, May 1st panic and lunch break.

- **What happens** (p.131–132) — Steps fearlessly between Yukimura and Hirata as Yukimura grabs Hirata's collar; gently envelops Yukimura’s clenched fist with both hands to disarm his fury.
- **Spoken** (p.131–132) — “Calm down, you two, okay? I’m sure that the teacher talked to us harshly so we’d be inspired to do better, right?… Besides, it’s only been one month since we started here, right? Like Hirata-kun said, it’s better if we all do our best together. Do you think that I’m wrong about that?”
- **Feat** (p.131–132) — Supreme emotional de-escalation: completely diffuses a physical altercation within seconds using gentle physical contact and moral appeal.
- **What happens** (p.134) — Verbally supports Hirata’s appeal from the podium when Sudō pushes back against rules.
- **Spoken** (p.134) — “Well, the teacher did say that the being late and talking in class were obviously bad, right?”
- **What happens** (p.138) — When Karuizawa casually hits her up for 2,000 private points to cover her deficit, Kushida agrees immediately without a shred of reluctance.
- **Spoken** (p.138) — “Okay, sure.”
- **What happens** (p.138–139) — Explains her philosophy to Ayanokōji when he warns that she will likely never see those points returned.
- **Spoken** (p.139) — “I can’t just ignore a friend in need. Karuizawa-san has a lot of friends, too, so I think it’s probably hard for her not to have any points.”
- **Spoken** (p.139) — “Wait, how do you transfer points?”
- **Spoken** (p.139) — “This school really does take great care of its students. It even has a way to help students like Karuizawa-san.”
- **What happens** (p.139) — Alerts Ayanokōji when the school PA summons him to the faculty office.
- **Spoken** (p.139) — “Looks like the teacher wants to see you.”
- **Trait** (p.138–139) — Pathological altruism / selfless facade: parts with her personal financial safety net instantly to preserve social harmony and project the perfect, supportive classmate persona.
- **Relationship · Karuizawa Kei** (p.138–139) — Acts as an unconditional financial and emotional safety valve.
- **Relationship · Hirata Yōsuke** (p.131–132, 134) — Integral co-mediator; provides the emotional anchor that backs up his structural leadership.

#### p.162–165, 173–175 — Recruiting the failures & library study session

Hallway outside Classroom 1-D; then school library.

- **What happens** (p.162–163) — Approached by Ayanokōji after school; eagerly agrees to help gather Sudō, Ike, and Yamauchi for Horikita's midterm study group.
- **Spoken** (p.162) — “Rejoice, Kushida. You’ve been selected as an ambassador of goodwill. Tomorrow, your hard work begins.” (AK to Kushida)
- **Spoken** (p.163) — “Well, I do want to get closer to her, but… well, I won’t worry about that now. Besides, it’s only natural to help out a friend in need.”
- **Spoken** (p.163) — “It’s cruel to expel someone for getting a bad grade. Isn’t it awful to have to say goodbye after you’ve worked to become friends with everyone?… I’ll do anything I can to be useful!”
- **What happens** (p.163) — Grasps Ayanokōji's hand warmly with an irresistible beaming smile.
- **What happens** (p.165) — Sets her condition: insists that she also be allowed to participate in the study group.
- **Spoken** (p.165) — “Ah, but there’s just one thing I want to ask you. I want to participate in the study group, too… Yes. I want to study with everyone.”
- **What happens** (p.165) — Asks Ayanokōji point-blank if he and Horikita are dating, reporting widespread rumors among the girls that the solitary Horikita only opens up to him.
- **Spoken** (p.165) — “This might be too forward, but are you two already dating?… It’s a big rumor among the girls in our class, you know. They say that even though Horikita-san is always alone, she only seems to get along really well with you, Ayanokouji-kun. And you guys eat together, after all.”
- **What happens** (p.165–166) — Successfully secures Ayanokōji's phone number.
- **What happens** (p.167) — Successfully mobilizes Ike, Yamauchi, and Sudō via text within hours using her feminine charm.
- **What happens** (p.173–175) — Arrives at the library wearing casual clothes; sits beside Horikita, brings snacks, and tries desperately to soothe the tension between Horikita and the failing boys.
- **What happens** (p.178–179) — When Horikita declares the failing boys are "dead weight" better off expelled, Kushida is deeply distressed.
- **Spoken** (p.178) — “Horikita-san, we’re not going to be able to study with anyone if things continue like this…”
- **Spoken** (p.179) — “Is it wrong? I don’t want to abandon Sudou-kun, Ike-kun, and Yamauchi-kun.”
- **What happens** (p.179) — Hurt and dismayed when Horikita accuses her of not genuinely caring about them; leaves the library resolved to find another way to save them.
- **Spoken** (p.179) — “What? I don’t understand. Why do you say things like that, Horikita-san? Why do you try to antagonize people? That’s…very sad.”

#### p.181–186 — Rooftop unmasking & the sexual assault blackmail frame

School rooftop and stairwell landing, dusk (around 6:00 PM).

- **What happens** (p.181–182) — Believing herself completely alone on the locked rooftop, drops her sweet, angelic persona entirely. Viciously vents raw, murderous hatred toward Horikita Suzune in a harsh, guttural voice.
- **Spoken** (p.182) — “Ahhh, so annoying!”
- **Spoken** (p.182) — “She’s seriously annoying! God, how irritating. It’d be better if she just died…”
- **Spoken** (p.182) — “Ugh, I hate stuck-up, snobby girls who think they’re so cute. Why is she such a harpy? A rotten girl like her couldn’t possibly tutor me.”
- **Spoken** (p.182) — “Ah, she’s the worst! She’s just the worst, the worst, the worst! Horikita, you’re so annoying! You’re so damn annoying!”
- **What happens** (p.182–183) — Hears Ayanokōji accidentally kick the rooftop door; instantly freezes, turns, and locks onto him with a chilling, predatory glare.
- **Spoken** (p.183) — “What…are you…doing here?”
- **Spoken** (p.183) — “Did you hear?”
- **What happens** (p.183) — Marches down the stairs, places her left forearm against Ayanokōji’s throat, and pins him hard against the wall; her tone, her actions, everything about her completely unlike the Kushida he knew (her expression almost comparable to Horikita’s).
- **Spoken** (p.183) — “If you tell anyone what you just heard, I won’t forgive you.” (her words were ice — not an idle threat)
- **Spoken** (p.183) — “In that case, I would tell everyone that you raped me,” she said.
- **Spoken** (p.183) — “That’s okay. It wouldn’t be false.” (her words had heft and power, leaving him unable to reply)
- **What happens** (p.183) — Grabs his right wrist, slowly opens his hand, and pushes his palm up against her breast, holding it there despite his attempts to pull away.
- **Spoken** (p.183) — “Your fingerprints are on my clothes. That’s evidence of my claim. I’m being serious. Understand?”
- **Spoken** (p.185) — “I’m going to leave this uniform in my room without washing it. If you betray me, I’ll hand it over to the police.”
- **Spoken** (p.185) — “It’s a promise,” she said. (steps away)
- **Spoken** (p.185) — “That’s none of your business.” (when AK asks which is the real her)
- **Spoken** (p.185) — “Is it bad to want everyone to like you? Do you understand how difficult it is to accomplish that? You can’t know, can you?” she asked.
- **Spoken** (p.185) — “At least on the surface, I wanted to appear to get along with Horikita.”
- **Spoken** (p.185) — “Yeah. That’s what I want out of life, though. That way, my existence has meaning.” (answered without hesitation; her internal rules demand she get close to Horikita)
- **Spoken** (p.185) — “Let me tell you something, while I have the chance. I absolutely despise gloomy, ordinary guys like you.”
- **Spoken** (p.186) — “What the… I don’t know what you mean. Did Horikita-san say something about me?” she snapped. (when AK probes whether they knew each other before school)
- **Spoken** (p.186) — “Just shut up. Hearing you talk irritates me, Ayanokouji-kun. I only want to know one thing. Do you swear you’ll never tell anyone what you learned here today?”
- **Spoken** (p.186) — “Okay. I believe you, Ayanokouji-kun.” (closes her eyes and slowly exhales)
- **Spoken** (p.186) — “You know, Ayanokouji-kun, you’re the only one that Horikita-san opens up to.”
- **What happens** (p.186) — After extracting his oath, the terrifying mask drops; she pivots smoothly into reflective analysis of Horikita as “the complete opposite” of herself.
- **Trait** (p.181–186) — Psychopathic dual personality / dark side: underneath the angelic, universally adored school idol lies a vicious, foul-mouthed sadist harboring burning hatred for anyone who rejects her (especially Horikita); chillingly pragmatic and capable of fabricating sexual assault charges with forensic precision to protect her secret.
- **Relationship · Horikita Suzune** (p.182) — Secret, venomous obsession and hatred; wishes she would die; considers her an unbearable, stuck-up harpy.
- **Relationship · Ayanokōji Kiyotaka** (p.183–186) — Traps him via manufactured sexual-assault evidence (fingerprints, unwashed uniform) and an unwinnable credibility contest; extracts a sworn oath, then reverts to warmth; establishes an uneasy, deadly truce.

#### p.187–188 — Landing & stairs: kicked thigh, railing, her beaming return

Same rooftop evening, continuing the trust-analysis exchange; then the breasts remark; she kicks his thigh; he grabs the railing.

- **Spoken · Kushida Kikyō** (p.187) — “Even so, she seems to trust you more than anyone else. Out of all the people I’ve ever met, Horikita seems the most wary of others and yet also the most self-confident. She certainly wouldn’t trust anyone worthless, even if they were unbelievably kind.”
- **Spoken · Kushida Kikyō** (p.187) — “That’s why I said I believed you. Ayanokouji-kun, you’re fundamentally indifferent to other people, aren’t you?”
- **Narration** (p.187) — “She kicked my thigh as hard she could. Panicking, I grabbed the railing.”
- **Spoken · Kushida Kikyō** (p.187) — “I kicked you because you said something stupid!”
- **What happens** (p.187) — Her stern expression melts into one of impatience; tromps back up the stairs, grabs her bag, then returns wearing a huge grin.
- **Spoken · Kushida Kikyō** (p.188) — “Let’s head back together,” (said brightly — the Jekyll and Hyde switch, back to sunny mode)

#### p.204–217, 226, 233–235 — Class A partnership, the ally test & the secret bond

- **What happens** (p.204) — Accepts Horikita's unprecedented lunch invitation at Palate; probes her motive ("Is it really for Sudou-kun and the others?") and is satisfied by the blunt selfish answer.
- **Spoken** (p.205) — “You’re free to think whatever you like. I just didn’t want you to try to lie to me. I’m glad you were honest. Okay, I’ll help. We’re classmates, after all. Right, Ayanokouji-kun?”
- **Spoken** (p.205) — “No, not for that. I want to work on getting into Class A with you. I want to help with everything else you’ll be doing.” (elevates her ask from study-group helper to Class A partner)
- **Spoken** (p.206) — “Really?! Yay!” (cheers; the three of them shake hands — her left and right arms extended to Horikita and AK)
- **What happens** (p.206) — Immediately takes charge of luring the boys back; Ike and Yamauchi arrive visibly on cloud nine from her invitations.
- **Spoken** (p.210–211) — Plays along with Ayanokōji's boyfriend bait by raising the stakes into a public motive: “W-well then, how about this? I’ll go on a date with whoever scores highest on the test. I like people who work really hard, even when they don’t like doing it.”
- **What happens** (p.213) — Waves Horikita off from across the room after ambushing AK into a lunch date — possibly engineered ("Had she planned that, by chance?"); keeps visible tabs on Ayanokōji post-rooftop.
- **Spoken** (p.214) — “Those girls are just being realists. You can’t afford to eat on dreams alone.” (on the third-year girls swarming Kouenji)
- **Spoken** (p.215) — “You’re ranked a respectable fifth place in the hot guy rankings. Congratulations! By the way, Satonaka-kun in Class A is in first place. Hirata-kun’s in second.” (relays the girls' anonymous ranking charts)
- **Spoken** (p.216) — “Talking to you is fun, Ayanokouji-kun.” / “Ha ha ha, yes, I did [hate you]. Sorry, but that’s how I really feel.” (smiling, unrepentant)
- **What happens** (p.216) — Reveals the true purpose of the lunch: an alliance stress-test between herself and Horikita.
- **Spoken** (p.216) — “If Horikita and I happen to clash, I hope that you’ll cooperate with me, Ayanokouji-kun.”
- **Spoken** (p.217) — “First, we’d need to build a relationship of mutual trust.” (declining to explain her situation until trust is established)
- **What happens** (p.226) — Accepts AK's errand: broadcast Chabashira's revised test-material list to all of Class D, including Hirata's group.
- **What happens** (p.233–235) — Co-conspirator on the old-test operation; agrees to take public credit as the receiver ("from a third-year student you're close to") so Ayanokōji stays invisible.
- **Spoken** (p.235) — “Don’t you feel that a strange bond of mutual trust forms between people who share a secret?”
- **Trait** (p.204–217) — Honest-about-being-manipulative mode around AK: alliance ambition, candid roasts, and veiled threats delivered under an unbroken smile.
- **Relationship · Horikita Suzune** (p.204–205) — Secures conditional partnership with the Class A project — her biggest proximity gain to date.
- **Relationship · Ayanokōji Kiyotaka** (p.216, 235) — Explicitly stakes a claim on his loyalty; wraps the old-test secret as mutual-trust bonding.

#### p.236–238 — The podium & "I really hate you"

- **What happens** (p.236) — Takes the classroom podium the day before the exam to distribute the printed old-test sheets — the role neither Horikita nor AK could play; total class attention (even Sudou stops).
- **Spoken** (p.236) — “Actually, these are the old test problems. I got them from a third-year student last night.” (the cover story lands perfectly)
- **Spoken** (p.236) — “Yes. To tell you the truth, I heard that the midterm test from the year before last had almost exactly the same problems as this one. So, if we study what’s on this test, it’ll surely come in handy.”
- **What happens** (p.237) — Downplays her own role when Horikita praises her.
- **Spoken** (p.237) — “I just did it for my friends. It was nothing special,”
- **What happens** (p.238) — Cornered by Horikita's direct "You hate me, don't you?", drops the pretense entirely and admits it with her smile intact.
- **Spoken** (p.238) — “Ha ha, you got me.” … “That’s right. I really hate you.”
- **Spoken** (p.238) — “Do you want me to tell you the reason?” (offering the reason unprompted — refused)
- **Trait** (p.236–238) — Public-relations genius + weaponized honesty: delivers the class's salvation from the podium while openly confirming hatred to her partner in the same afternoon.
- **Relationship · Horikita Suzune** (p.238) — The relationship's true baseline established: confessed mutual hatred under a functioning alliance.

#### p.246 — Relief at the results

- **What happens** (p.246) — Shares a glance and sighs of relief with Ayanokōji at the posted results — until the red line appears.

#### p.256–261 — The Class A recruitment pitch

- **What happens** (p.256) — Praises Ayanokōji's plain dorm room on request ("It’s simple, but it feels nice and clean.").
- **Spoken** (p.257) — “Everything was thanks to Horikita-san’s efforts. She tutored Ike, Yamauchi, and Sudou.” (deflects credit onto Horikita in front of the trio)
- **What happens** (p.258–259) — Escalates the gathering: reveals the Class A project to Ike, Yamauchi, and Sudou and formally recruits them as helpers.
- **Spoken** (p.258) — “We’re all friends here, after all. Horikita-san, Ayanokouji-kun, and I are all working together to get into Class A. If you’re okay with it, I want you three to help us,”
- **Spoken** (p.258) — “Yes. I absolutely am. Increasing our points is an inevitable part of getting to the top as well.”
- **Spoken** (p.261) — Proverb war: “A single arrow is easily broken, but not ten in a bundle,” — then, after Horikita knocks the trio down: “W-well how about this? Three heads are better than one! Or something like that,”
- **Spoken** (p.261) — “If we keep going back and forth like this, though, we won’t get anywhere. It’s definitely better for us to get along.” (forces the truce that lands the recruits)
- **Trait** (p.256–261) — Morale engine: every time Horikita demoralizes the trio, Kushida rebuilds them — the recruitment pairing that makes the group functional.

### Year 1, Volume 2

#### p.19 — Ch.2 sec. 2.2: the social ceiling, narrated

- **Backstory** (p.19) — AK's snapshot of her position: "She’s extremely popular and has many friends, both girls and boys. She gets in-person invitations, along with constant invites over the phone and through email... when she eats with friends she appears to have a real life."

#### p.24 — Ch.2 sec. 2.3: group-chat wistfulness

Kushida was the one who doubted Horikita would ever join the class group chat after the midterm invite ("Kushida had doubted whether Horikita would actually participate, since she hated talking with others.").

#### p.27–38 — Ch.2 sec. 2.4: Sudou's crisis conference — angel mode with an icy seam

Knocks during AK's kettle moment; "Kushida, the Madonna of Class D, poked her head through the entrance. She looked as cute as ever."

- **Spoken** (p.28) — "Oh, Sudou-kun’s already here,"
- **Spoken** (p.28) — "Yeah, I do. That’s so we can meet up here… Wait, did you not know about that, Ayanokouji-kun?" (she also holds a duplicate key to his room — assumed he'd consented)
- **Spoken** (p.28) — "Umm, well…should I return it to you?" (apologetically offers the key back)
- **Spoken** (p.29) — "S-suspended?"
- **Spoken** (p.30) — "Oh, I got it, Ayanokouji-kun. He viciously beat Chabashira-sensei and then he spit on her!"
- **Spoken** (p.30) — "Ha ha, I’m only joking! Sudou-kun wouldn’t go that far." (the joke lands wrong — Sudou's shock proves the secret is real)
- **Spoken** (p.30) — "Just wait a minute, Sudou-kun. Can you please start over, and go a bit more slowly?"
- **Spoken** (p.31) — "A regular player? That’s amazing, Sudou-kun! Congratulations!"
- **Spoken** (p.31) — "Still, that’s amazing. We’ve only just started school."
- **Spoken** (p.31) — "So then they painted you as the bad guy, Sudou-kun."
- **Spoken** (p.31) — "If Class C started this, then Sudou-kun isn’t at fault."
- **Spoken** (p.31) — "We should tell Chabashira-sensei tomorrow. We should tell her it wasn’t Sudou-kun’s fault,"
- **Spoken** (p.32) — "Sudou-kun, you didn’t lie to the teacher, did you? I mean, it seems strange. They didn’t believe you even though you said you didn’t do anything wrong. Right?"
- **Spoken** (p.33) — "I guess that their plan is to severely punish Sudou for punching them, huh?"
- **Spoken** (p.34) — "Let’s just come out and ask those three Class C students to be honest. If they feel like what they did was wrong, then surely they’d feel guilty about it. Right?"
- **Spoken** (p.34) — "There are two ways we can prove your innocence, Sudou-kun. The first way is to simply get those boys in Class C to admit that they lied. Since you weren’t at fault, it’s probably best to get them to acknowledge that."
- **Spoken** (p.35) — "The other idea is to find the eyewitness you talked about, Sudou-kun. If someone happened to see the fight, then we should be able to get to the heart of the matter."
- **Spoken** (p.35) — "Asking people one by one? Or we could simply address each class as a whole,"
- **Spoken** (p.35) — "H-huh? You don’t want us to tell anyone?"
- **Spoken** (p.35) — "But won’t the students in Class C talk about how Sudou-kun was violent? I mean, that would work in their favor, I’d think."
- **Spoken** (p.35) — "Sudou-kun, I think it would be better if you kept your distance," (Sudou must stay away while accused)
- **Spoken** (p.36) — "I don’t think it’s being dumped on us. We want you to rely on us, Sudou-kun. I don’t know how much we can do, but we’ll try our best. Okay?"
- **Spoken** (p.36) — "I have a few things I wanted to talk to you about, Ayanokouji-kun. You didn’t seem very enthusiastic about helping Sudou-kun."
- **Spoken** (p.36) — "Maybe, but Sudou-kun came to you, Ayanokouji-kun. He came to you before Horikita-san, Hirata-kun, or even Ike-kun."
- **Feat** (p.37) — The mask slips mid-conversation: "For an instant, Kushida’s glare turned icy, which perplexed me." — then she recomposes into advice.
- **Spoken** (p.37) — "I think it might be better if you made more effort to blend in, Ayanokouji-kun,"
- **Spoken** (p.37) — "Of course. We’re friends. So what will you do, Ayanokouji-kun?"
- **Spoken** (p.37) — "Do you think Horikita-san will help us?"
- **Spoken** (p.37) — "I know that you’re trying to dodge the question, but you’ll help, too. Won’t you, Ayanokouji-kun?"
- **Spoken** (p.37) — "You won’t be useless. I’m sure you’ll be useful, somehow."
- **Spoken** (p.37) — "So what should we do? Sudou-kun said that it wouldn’t help, but I think it’d be good to talk with the three students he fought. To tell you the truth, I’m friends with Komiya-kun and the others. Therefore, it might be possible to persuade them. Hmm, it may be dangerous, though."
- **Spoken** (p.38) — "Well then, I guess looking for the eyewitness is our best bet."
- **Trait** (p.29–38) — Structured empathy: jokes to defuse, then imposes an agenda (two proof routes), volunteers her cross-class network (Komiya et al. in C) as a tool, and pins AK to a commitment he keeps dodging.
- **Limit** (p.37) — Her pressure only works socially — AK admits "Like right now, I didn’t have the guts to promise that I’d help."; her icy glare is the only lever that lands.
- **Relationship · Sudō Ken** (p.35–36) — Officially takes his side, secures his cooperation with warmth, keeps the secrecy bargain.
- **Relationship · Ayanokōji Kiyotaka** (p.36–38) — Escalates one-on-one after Sudou leaves; demands he publicly invest in the case; he internally recalls her "I hate you" warning while complying outwardly.

#### p.41–43 — Ch.3 open: she takes the class's anger apart in front of everyone

With the uproar starting to get out of hand, AK's note on her motive: "Naturally, Kushida didn’t want this to happen." — she is defending the class's cohesion, not Sudou. She tells the room Sudou's whole account — the regular-player nomination, the envious clubmates, the threat, the self-defence: "Kushida conveyed yesterday’s story to the whole class."

- **Spoken** (p.42) — “Everyone, can you please listen to what I have to say?”
- **Spoken** (p.42) — “What our teacher said seems to be true. Sudou-kun may have been in a fight. However, Sudou-kun was dragged into it.”
- **Spoken** (p.42) — “I’d like to ask you all again. If anyone knows someone who saw what happened, whether it’s someone in class, a friend, or an upperclassman, please tell me. You can contact me at any time. I would sincerely appreciate it.”
- **Feat** (p.42–43) — AK measures the difference between her and the teacher saying the same thing: "Even though she’d basically said the same thing as Chabashira-sensei, the class had a very different response. She had an innate ability to connect with people. Her presence glowed so bright that I could almost feel it."
- **Limit** (p.43) — Charisma alone does not carry it: after Yamauchi's accusation, "Kushida’s appeal for Sudou’s innocence didn’t seem to have reached anyone." It takes Hirata standing up — and then Karuizawa — to turn the room.
- **Relationship · Hirata Yōsuke** (p.43) — He backs her appeal publicly; AK credits the pair of them plus Karuizawa with converting the class, which is the closest the volume comes to the two leaders acting together.
- **Relationship · Karuizawa Kei** (p.43) — AK's paired mechanism, stated in the same breath: "If Kushida lived by the softness of her heart, then Karuizawa lived by the strength of her will."

#### p.44, p.47 — Ch.3 sec. 3.1: keeping Ayanokōji inside the group

She assembles the lunch table — AK, Horikita, Ike, Yamauchi, Sudou — by asking him directly: "When lunchtime came, Kushida had invited me with a smile. She’d said, 'Want to get lunch?' I’d said okay, of course. I mean, I didn’t have any choice in the matter." She leaves the table early to work her own network: “Ah, sorry. I have to go for a bit. I’m going to try asking my upperclassmen friends if they saw anything.”

#### p.54–63 — Ch.3 sec. 3.2–3.3: Horikita, the lesson, and Class B

She takes AK's arm in front of Ike and Yamauchi and walks him out with her; Ike and Yamauchi are left behind because, as AK notes, "You certainly couldn’t claim those two were friendly with Horikita." In the Class B room she is the only one of the four who moves: "Kushida was the only one who remained unfazed. In fact, she found her Class B friends and, with a smile on her face, waved to them and headed over." She pitches Sudou's case to the roughly ten Class B students still in the room — the first cross-class advocacy of the volume.

- **Spoken** (p.54) — “Are you really heading back? Horikita-san?”
- **Spoken** (p.54) — “You will…help us out, won’t you?” (stopping AK from slipping out; AK: "I couldn’t refuse. Slightly upturned eyes + pleading = lethal.")
- **Spoken** (p.55) — “I want to get Horikita-san to help us out this time. Can you try asking her again?”
- **Spoken** (p.55) — “Yeah. I want to chase after her. If Horikita-san helps, I think she’ll make a big difference.”
- **Spoken** (p.56) — “I really want you to help us with Sudou’s case. Is that possible?”
- **Spoken** (p.56) — “I know you did, but…but, I think this is necessary to reach Class A.”
- **Spoken** (p.57) — “W-wait. What do you mean? Wasn’t Sudou-kun dragged into the fight?”
- **Spoken** (p.57) — “Sudou-kun is a perpetrator? Is that…true?”
- **Spoken** (p.60) — “I don’t agree with abandoning Sudou-kun just to punish him. If she’s dissatisfied with him, I think she should at least talk to him directly. That’s what friends would do.”
- **Spoken** (p.60) — “Okay. I understand. I’ll follow your advice, Ayanokouji-kun.”
- **Trait** (p.60) — AK's definition of her, offered as a limit on himself: "She would extend her hand to a friend in need as many times as it took. It sounds simple, but it was actually difficult. Only someone like Kushida could do such a thing."
- **Feat** (p.60) — She is moved mid-conversation and lets it show: "Kushida arched her back and stretched; I’d changed her thinking." — then converts the new position into action ("Okay, let’s go hunt for the witness.")
- **Spoken** (p.61) — “No, I’m sorry. I failed,”
- **Spoken** (p.61) — “Well then, let’s go to Class B right away!”
- **Spoken** (p.62) — “I also think you’re being too negative. I do think it’s a good idea to ask Class B, but I also think we should ask several people at once. If not, we may not find the witness in time.”
- **Spoken** (p.62) — “I’m sorry, Ayanokouji-kun.” (overruling his one-at-a-time plan with Ike and Yamauchi behind her, and apologising for it; AK withdraws: "Kushida’s plan had convinced me, so I withdrew my own.")
- **Feat** (p.63) — AK's note on the transferability of her manner: "She chatted with people regardless of gender, exactly how she acted in Class D. No one was more jealous of this than Ike and Yamauchi."
- **Limit** (p.63) — Her network has a ceiling she cannot see: AK's whole plan for Class B rests on the class's self-interest, and she is running on friendship, which is why the report she brings back surprises him. (What the report is, the text withholds until the next chapter.)

#### p.66–82 — Ch.4 sec. 4.0–4.1: she runs the search, and is the only one who can name Sakura's seat

Horikita arrives at the room and Kushida is the reason she stays: "Kushida peered over at the entrance, probably concerned that Horikita wouldn’t return. 'Ah, Horikita-san!' Kushida said. Kushida smiled and waved to Horikita." She is the class's map of itself: "No, you’re both wrong. She sits diagonally ahead of Sudou-kun to the right." — AK: "Kushida corrected them, a slightly pouty look on her face."

- **Spoken** (p.66) — "As long as we can find a witness, it’s not impossible. Let’s keep trying our best, Ike-kun." — the pep line she gives the boy who has just declared the case hopeless.
- **Feat** (p.74) — Her method of remembering is exact: "It’s certainly true that Sudou-kun didn’t say he saw anyone. He said that he had a feeling that someone was there." — she corrects the class's summary of a claim three days old.
- **Spoken** (p.75) — She re-engineers the search from the witness to the witness's surroundings: "We might want to change our methods a little. For example, let’s look for someone who might have found a witness." — AK's judgement: "That wasn’t a bad idea."
- **Spoken** (p.76) — "If you didn’t come here to help, then why did you come?" — and then, instead of pressing: "Well, I’m happy if you just want to listen to us talk. I hoped you could give some advice."
- **Spoken** (p.80) — She defends a classmate she has never spoken to, in public, against Ike: "It’s not good to remember people based on something like that, Ikekun! That’s pathetic."
- **Spoken** (p.80) — The phone attempt fails and she names why it is a delicate thing: "No luck. The call didn’t go through. I can try again later, but this may be a delicate matter." and "She gave me her contact information, but she doesn’t know me very well. She might be confused if I tried to call her."
- **Spoken** (p.82) — She reads the room's failure before it happens and shrinks the plan to one person: "Well, I’ll try to talk to her myself tomorrow. I think she might be on her guard if a lot of people approach her."
- **Relationship · Sakura Airi** (p.80–82) — She holds Sakura's number and Sakura's seating position and none of Sakura's trust, and is the one student whose name she will not let Ike reduce to a body.

#### p.86–93 — Ch.4 sec. 4.2–4.3: the approach fails in front of the whole class

"Class was over. Kushida stood up right as homeroom ended and walked over towards Sakura, who was quietly preparing to leave. Kushida seemed oddly nervous." She takes the blame for a broken camera that was not hers: "I-I’m sorry. It’s because I came up and talked to you so suddenly, that…" — and is answered flatly: "No, that’s not it. I was just careless, that’s all… Anyway, goodbye."

- **Spoken** (p.86) — "Sakura-san?" then "I wanted to ask you something, Sakura-san. Do you have a moment? It’s about Sudou’s case."
- **Spoken** (p.88) — "Can you please make some time? This is important, so I’d like to talk to you. When Sudou-kun was involved in the incident, were you perhaps somewhere nearby?"
- **Spoken** (p.88) — "Can’t you please spare just a few minutes now?" — the third ask, the one that turns the conversation into a scene the room is watching.
- **Limit** (p.88) — AK's post-mortem of her judgement, in her own terms: "Kushida probably had expected this conversation to go more smoothly, and had gone into this situation thinking she wasn’t going to be rejected. That explained why this was currently falling apart."
- **Feat** (p.91) — Even after failing she keeps the case alive by asking the quietest boy in the room: "Ayanokouji-kun, you’re Sudou-kun’s ally, aren’t you?" — "Well, it’s just that things seem to a bit tense. Everyone’s desire to save Sudou is fading."
- **Relationship · Ayanokōji Kiyotaka** (p.91) — She uses his proximity as a resource twice in one day: the ally question, and the earlier instruction that he keep Horikita reachable (Hirata briefs AK about her because "she doesn’t talk to anyone except for you").

#### p.128–131 — Ch.4 sec. 4.8: she phones him at night, and cannot finish what she starts

The first time she reaches him unbidden, after ten: "I’m sorry for calling you so late. You’re still up?" she asked.

- **Spoken** (p.128) — "Sakura-san’s digital camera was broken, right? I feel like I’m partially to blame since I made her so flustered. So I wanted to take responsibility for that…"
- **Spoken** (p.129) — "Yeah. She seemed hesitant at first, but then said that she’d be fine with the day after tomorrow. I think that Sakura’s digital camera is probably really important to her."
- **Spoken** (p.129) — "If we were just going to get it repaired, I suppose. But, there’s another thing. I’d like your help with it, Ayanokouji-kun."
- **Spoken** (p.129) — What Horikita has concluded, carried by the one person Horikita will not speak to: "Horikita-san seems convinced that Sakura saw everything. After getting a bit closer to Sakura-san, I also think she knows something. But there has to be some reason why she’s keeping quiet, since she keeps denying that she witnessed it."
- **Spoken** (p.129) — "That reminds me. It was really scary when Kouenji-kun and Sudoukun looked like they were about to start fighting." — printed as one word; kept as the translation has it.
- **Spoken** (p.129) — "I couldn’t even move. Hirata-kun was so amazing, though."
- **Spoken** (p.130) — "Class D was able to come together, thanks to you and Hirata. The fact that the boys and girls are separated plays a big part, too."
- **Spoken** (p.130) — "Speaking of special, don’t you think that Horikita-san is way more special than someone like me? She’s great at studying and excels at sports. It makes me wonder why she’s in Class D."
- **Spoken** (p.130) — "But doesn’t she behave normally with you, Ayanokouji-kun?"
- **Spoken** (p.131) — "Huh? Ah, yeah, it is. Why? What about it?" — he has asked whether her room is on the ninth floor.
- **Feat** (p.130) — Her gift with the class's outliers, described from the outside: "Kushida had done a wonderful job taking the proper first steps to earn Sakura’s trust."
- **Limit** (p.131) — The only silence the volume records her in: "Suddenly, Kushida fell silent. It was unexpected silence, without warning. Our conversation, which had been smooth up until now, came to a screeching halt."
- **Limit** (p.131) — She starts a confession three times and stops: "Hey—" then "I-If… Well… I… I—" then "No, it’s nothing." — and the recorder's verdict: "That sure didn’t seem like nothing…"
- **Spoken** (p.131) — "Well, I’ll see you the day after tomorrow, Ayanokouji-kun." — the call's last line, with the unfinished one still in the air.
- **Trait** (p.130–131) — She is the only classmate who tests his account of Horikita instead of accepting it — and his reaction to being read is discomfort rather than deflection: "I didn’t like being misunderstood by Kushida."
- **Caveat** (p.131) — She hesitates immediately after being asked for her floor number, and the volume never says whether the two are connected. What is recorded is only the sequence: the question, the halt, the swallowed sentence, the goodbye.

#### p.132–145 — Ch.4 sec. 4.9–4.10: how she works on someone who cannot be worked on

- **Spoken** (p.132) — "Good morning!" and "Sorry. Were you waiting long?"
- **Spoken** (p.132) — "This is the first time we’ve met up on a day off. It’s refreshing."
- **Spoken** (p.133) — The opening test of whether he has been excluded from his own class: "Weren’t you really busy during your downtime last week? I’m glad that you came despite that, Ayanokouji-kun." — then, precisely: "I’m talking about Ike-kun and the others going to that café, of course."
- **Spoken** (p.133) — "By any chance…"
- **Spoken** (p.133) — "I didn’t mean anything… I’m sorry, I guess I said something wrong…" — and when he says he does not care: "You do seem to care…"
- **Spoken** (p.135) — "No, no, that’s not true at all. I think we’re great together," — to his "I think I might look a little out of place next to you."
- **Spoken** (p.135) — "You’re that surprisingly delicate, Ayanokouji-kun? I don’t really care what others say. I don’t think it’s an insult at all. I really think we suit each other."
- **Spoken** (p.136) — "She asked me to invite you, Ayanokouji-kun. Didn’t Sakura-san contact you?" — Sakura's invitation came through Kushida, and Kushida did not know he had not been contacted directly.
- **Spoken** (p.136) — "Right now, how about we sit and wait?"
- **Spoken** (p.138) — "Let’s see, I’m sure they have a repair counter somewhere. Let’s check it out."
- **Spoken** (p.138) — "There it is! The repairs counter."
- **Spoken** (p.138) — "What’s the matter, Sakura-san?" — she is the only one of the three who asks about Sakura's fear out loud.
- **Feat** (p.139) — The transaction as an act of translation: "Kushida’s worldly wisdom was certainly impressive. Even though it was her first time meeting the store clerk, she was soon talking to him as if they were longtime friends."
- **Feat** (p.139) — She reads the clerk's interest and manages it without refusing him: "In order to conclude their business, she urged Sakura to hand over the camera."
- **Spoken** (p.140) — "That clerk sure was something else. He talked with such incredible passion, I was just so shocked,"
- **Spoken** (p.141) — "N-no, I wasn’t disgusted by him. Do you know something? About that clerk?" — the question that lets Sakura say she was afraid.
- **Spoken** (p.141) — "Did you know about this, Ayanokouji-kun?" — "Kushida seemed to have an epiphany. She turned to me with wide eyes."
- **Spoken** (p.141) — "You really looked out for Sakura-san,"
- **Spoken** (p.142) — "Oh, no need to thank me." and then "Sakura-san, you really like your camera. Don’t you?"
- **Spoken** (p.142) — "I think being knowledgeable and liking things are separate matters. It’s wonderful to be so passionate about something."
- **Spoken** (p.144) — "Sakura-san, have we met somewhere before?" — "Kushida murmured."
- **Spoken** (p.144) — The ask that Sakura refuses: "Sorry. It’s just that when I look at you, I get this feeling that we’ve met before, Sakura-san. Hey, weird question, but could you try removing your glasses?"
- **Spoken** (p.144) — "We should hang out together again, Sakura-san. Not just with me, but with my other friends, too."
- **Spoken** (p.145) — Her offer of a normal relationship, phrased as a complaint about speech: "you can talk to us normally, you know? If it’s okay with you. We’re in the same grade. It sounds a little weird when you speak so formally to us."
- **Spoken** (p.145) — "It’s not really a bad thing! I mean, I’d be happy if you didn’t speak so formally to me, though."
- **Spoken** (p.145) — "It’s okay, though. You don’t need to force yourself."
- **Spoken** (p.146) — The ask that finally gets the witness: "So, does that mean that you saw Sudou-kun fight with those other students?" and then "No, we do. Why did you decide to tell us now, though? I mean, I’m happy you did, but I don’t want you to force yourself. You don’t have to do this just because you’re grateful, you know?"
- **Spoken** (p.146) — "Is that really true? You’re not forcing yourself?" Kushida asked. — the same question twice in one minute, because the first answer was a shake of the head.
- **Spoken** (p.146) — "Thank you, Sakura-san. I’m sure Sudou-kun will be really happy." — "Kushida took Sakura’s hand."
- **Feat** (p.146) — The case the whole class failed to move in three weeks is closed by a hand-hold and two refusals to press: "I wondered if a new friendship had been born right here, right now. At any rate, we had Sudou’s witness."
- **Trait** (p.132–146) — She is the only person in Class D who gets things done by being with people rather than by being right about them; the volume records her succeeding at exactly the task Horikita wrote off as worthless.
- **Relationship · Sakura Airi** (p.138–146) — From a camera knocked over in a classroom (p.90) to a promise to testify in a shopping mall: the whole distance is crossed by Kushida refusing to make anything harder than it already is.
- **Relationship · Ayanokōji Kiyotaka** (p.128–145) — She recruits him by asking, tests whether he has been left out of his own friend group, and lets him fill in a form; he is her one witness that she is capable of a plain conversation.
- **Limit** (p.144) — Her instinct to close on Sakura stops short of the truth: she asks about the glasses, gets refused, and "Kushida seemed to feel that it would make trouble if she pressed the issue, so she kept quiet. Or rather, she didn’t ask anything else."

#### p.147–150 — Ch.4 sec. 4.11: the same evening, on two calls

- **Spoken** (p.147) — "We got closer to Sakura, but… Is it really okay for me to say that?" — her voice on the phone that night; the translation leaves the reply's speaker untagged and it is not assigned.
- **Spoken** (p.147) — Her reason for the glasses, which turns out not to be a reason: "Well, I mean… I thought saying so might be kind of cruel, but… I just feel like her glasses don’t really suit her, for some reason. - **Spoken** (p.148) — "There’s that. Wearing glasses does make you look smart."
- **Spoken** (p.148) — "I knew it was right to bring you along, Ayanokouji-kun. I feel like you’re very observant of people."

#### p.10–12 — Ch.1 sec. 1: Kushida Kikyou’s Soliloquy

Cold-open soliloquy: Kushida narrates the ideal self she built, the junior-high incident that broke it, and the bus meeting that reopened the wound.

- **Spoken · Kushida Kikyō** (p.10) — “Do people live according to their own ideals, I wonder? Well, I do.”
- **Spoken · Kushida Kikyō** (p.10) — “Wouldn’t you say I’m perfect?”
- **Spoken · Kushida Kikyō** (p.10) — “Of course, I wouldn’t say that I am. There are girls cuter than me, and many who are smarter or more athletic. That’s obvious. Yes, that’s quite obvious.”
- **Inner voice** (p.10) — “I hate losing far more than the average person does.”
- **Inner voice** (p.10) — “I once vomited from the intense emotional stress that losing caused me.”
- **Trait** (p.10) — Genius-child praise until junior high, when peers overtook her; she hunted for something she could never lose at.

#### p.212–215 — Ch.8: the public persona, the knife, the last time

Serious look in the dorm room; forceful under the friendly tone while eyes and ears are out at dinner.

- **Spoken · Kushida Kikyō** (p.212) — “Look, Horikita-san. To be honest with you, I don’t think you’re seeing the current situation for what it is.”

- **Spoken · Kushida Kikyō** (p.213) — “were thinking about your class, shouldn’t you keep your personal feelings out of it?”
- **Spoken · Kushida Kikyō** (p.213) — “You’ve made me, and me alone, your priority. Winning and losing are just secondary concerns. Am I wrong?”
- **Spoken · Kushida Kikyō** (p.213) — “So you admit it.”
- **Spoken · Kushida Kikyō** (p.213) — “It doesn’t matter what you do,”
- **Spoken · Kushida Kikyō** (p.213) — “Enough already. I want you to understand that.”
- **Spoken · Kushida Kikyō** (p.213) — “Look, it’s not my place to say this, but have you forgotten about how you dragged me out in front of the student council president and made me swear I wouldn’t do anything? Putting aside my own feelings, which I will not get over, I promised I wouldn’t do anything to sabotage you, Horikita-san. I thought you’d at least trust me that much. Or did you think I’d break my promise right away?”
- **Spoken · Kushida Kikyō** (p.214) — “Wow, you’re blunt.”
- **Spoken · Kushida Kikyō** (p.214) — “No matter what, I will not say anything to anyone about your past. What do I have to do to make you believe me?”
- **Spoken · Kushida Kikyō** (p.214) — “Unfortunately, there’s no way you can convince me,”
- **Spoken · Kushida Kikyō** (p.214) — “You’re certainly right about that. If I did find out you told someone, I’d show no mercy. I might even destroy the entire class, just like I did back in junior high. As someone who’s aiming for Class A, you obviously wouldn’t put yourself in jeopardy. At least, that’s what you’d assume,”
- **Spoken · Kushida Kikyō** (p.214) — “But if you ask me, our current environment’s a little cramped.”
- **Spoken · Kushida Kikyō** (p.214) — “For example, would you obey a stranger who puts a knife to the back of your neck and says he’ll hurt you if you don’t cooperate? There’s a difference between a situation where you can’t be hurt even if someone tried to and a situation where someone could hurt you if they wanted to. You understand, don’t you?”
- **Spoken · Kushida Kikyō** (p.215) — “Do you honestly not get that your know-it-all tone irritates me more than anything else? You’re talking this way because you know my true personality. I can’t stand that. If you didn’t know anything about me, you wouldn’t be saying these things.”
- **Spoken · Kushida Kikyō** (p.215) — “You’re smarter than me. Wouldn’t you do just fine even at another school? Besides, you came here so you could be at the same school as your older brother. Right, Horikita-san? But your brother will be graduating soon, so you won’t need to force yourself to stay. You could study at a different school, go on to college or land a job. Wouldn’t that be fine?”
- **Spoken · Kushida Kikyō** (p.215) — “I’ll be quiet for now. But I will never trust or cooperate with you, Horikita-san. Until either you or I are gone from this school, we’ll never be okay. No matter how many times we have this conversation. You’d do well to remember that.”
- **Spoken · Kushida Kikyō** (p.215) — “Not just today. This is the last time.”

#### p.186–200 — The midnight negotiation

Comes to Ayanokōji’s room at midnight February 11 with Valentine chocolate. Ayanokōji asks for personal information about first-years to stop the Ichinose rumors; she resists betraying friends. Ayanokōji explains the plan to escalate rumors so the school cannot sit back; Class A would be first suspect. Ayanokōji reveals he knows she is recording (phone and backup recorder); she stops the recording. Asks for Horikita’s withdrawal as her condition; Ayanokōji refuses; he offers half of all future private points. Accepts the deal; takes out a backup voice recorder; Ayanokōji’s defense is making himself useful to her. Begins sharing secrets: crushes, hatreds, family circumstances, past delinquency. Explains the surface group chat versus the hidden group chat that badmouths excluded members; dozens of such two-faced groups; leaves.

- **Spoken** (p.186) — “Sorry for calling you at such a bad time. If you don’t mind, please come in.”
- **Spoken** (p.187) — “I’m on good terms with Ichinose-san as well. If there’s anything I can do for her, I’d want to do that.”
- **Spoken** (p.187) — “I can’t just betray my friends.”
- **Spoken** (p.189) — “So you already knew? That I’ve been recording?”
- **Spoken** (p.190) — “Horikita Suzune’s withdrawal from school.”
- **Spoken** (p.190) — “Unacceptable.”
- **Spoken** (p.191) — “Alright, I’m on board.”
- **Spoken** (p.192) — “I have all the evidence I need right here. If you betray me at all… you know what will happen, yes?”
- **Spoken** (p.195) — “For example, there’s this group chat, we’ll call it group A… there’s a second group chat, made by the same girls. Let’s call that one group B.”
- **Spoken** (p.195) — “It’s late, I’m going home. I’m looking forward to the outcome of our agreement, Ayanokōji-kun.”
- **Relationship · Ayanokōji Kiyotaka** (p.186–195) — Negotiates for her secrets; accepts half his future private points and provides both recordings as insurance.

#### p.70–75 — Kushida visits Yagami: the conspiracy for the exam

Visits first-year Takuya Yagami in the first-year dormitory; he recorded (via a phone recording of) Chabashira's exam briefing and played it for her. Yagami believes he's counted the exam's only condition for expulsion — the cumulative voting penalty — but interprets that there are other ways; he expects the very question that divides the class and has already drafted the play: make her steer the class to crown Horikita as expulsion candidate. Yagami grips her shoulders (she reflexively tries to flee; he overwhelms her); she plays along but is frightened by how firmly he has her cornered — she neither wants nor refuses the expulsions; he releases her.

- **Spoken** (p.74) — "You think I can do it...?"

- **Spoken** (p.74) — "I want to expel Horikita, I want to expel Ayanokōji. I'm definitely going to get them expelled...!"

#### p.172–215 — Kushida's mask: the confession, the collapse, the purge

In the eye-contact interrogation; calm as anyone else; the class trusts her with their secrets. Her confession sequence — she wails about regretting her choice; begs the class to understand her; blames Ayanokōji's decision; then the offer — "Ayanokōji, who was the leader of this special exam, Horikita-san... who urged people to vote in favour, should take responsibility for that." Spams the class with accusations about Kiyotaka's leadership; then volunteers herself as the candidate — "Enough is enough... I can't take it anymore, my heart can't take it any more...!" The last stand — her fake crying begs sympathy; then the purge: she rips through the class's secrets one by one — Shinohara's suitors (Ike/Komiya), Mii-chan's crush on Hirata, Haruka's crush on Kiyotaka, Satō's group dynamics. Horikita confronts her — the class protects her for a moment; Horikita reaffirms her support for her; Kushida screams at Horikita, accusing her of betrayal; begs for mercy; can't face the truth. Total collapse — tears, trembling, repeated "Regret! Regret! Regret!" (TL note flags the ambiguity: regretting her actions in her previous school, her failure to persuade the class, or her treatment of Mii-chan — kept as printed).

- **Spoken** (p.171) — "Hey, hey. I guess we'll just have to vote in favour, won't we?" — no, that's an unnamed student

- **Spoken** (p.195) — "Enough is enough... I can't take it anymore, my heart can't take it any more..."

- **Spoken** (p.204) — "Fufu... I've been a fool, haven't I? That deal was a mistake..."
- **Spoken** (p.205) — "In the next vote I will be expelled. This class will get class points because of my sacrifice, huh?"
- **Trait** (p.172–215) — The master of false tears — weaponizes the class's secrets she held; the class survives her by one hour; her last act is public self-contempt.

### Year 2, Volume 6

#### p.15–29 — Ch.1: Absent — the class fracture's center

AK narrates the exam's aftermath: her explosive revelations after being cornered created cracks everywhere; she was pushed to the edge of expulsion when Horikita declared her an essential resource; the promise to expel only the traitor had already shaken the class. She has holed up in her room since the exam weekend — Horikita has been waiting outside it from dawn, ringing the doorbell without a sound; humiliation of a lifetime; she stores food; she will not leave her room even once; dark circles on her watcher's face.

- **Mentioned** (p.28) — Absent from the Monday homeroom — “Kushida, Hasebe and Wan – three students are absent. That’s unusual.” Chabashira has not heard from her; she is the only one of the three who has not called in.
- **Mentioned** (p.59) — Still absent Tuesday; Chabashira has not been able to reach her; the class's 380-point starting handicap vs the absentee-less 400 is noted by AK.
- **Caveat** (p.24–25) — AK notes Kushida has not repented and is likely to stay uncooperative — her information-leak potential and the class's instability are live issues.
- **Relationship · Horikita Suzune** (p.20–29) — Horikita is at her door from dawn; she defended Kushida in the exam; the class blames Horikita for her absence.
- **Relationship · Ayanokōji Kiyotaka** (p.15–17) — He drove her to the edge of expulsion; Horikita's counter-move spared her.
- **Relationship · Hasebe Haruka** (p.28) — Both absent; her absence is from Airi's expulsion, Kushida's from her own revelations — the class's two wounds.
- **Relationship · Wang Mei-Yu** (p.28) — Also absent; the third wound.

#### p.112–125 — Ch.3: Kushida's week — the room; the buzzer; Ibuki's assault (Horikita's POV)

A week holed up in her room following the special exam — never seen by Horikita since Monday; store-bought landline-ish silence; the electrical meter spins fast — she is inside and using electronics; Ibuki corners her with crude verbal assault and foot-banging — she opens the door (“───You’re annoying me, so could you stop it, Ibuki-san?”). Sunday's marathon pays off: Monday she arrives late — first time ever late — “I’m sorry, I’m late.” — sits, answers smoothly, keeps her eyes forward; after homeroom: pre-empts Horikita's move and stands; addresses Mii-chan first; apologizes to Shinohara, Matsushita and the others; announces her continued information-war stance only for herself; promises one thing — unless she falls, no more secrecy exposures; will graduate in Class A; “I’m doing it for myself.” — she tries to speak to Haruka, who does not respond; the Angel is dead.

#### p.273–285 — Ch.5: Kushida's festival — the debt to Amasawa (Horikita's POV)

She rushes to Horikita-Ibuki — “Finally, found you.” — her eyes are on Amasawa-san; she follows her; she recruits Rokkaku-san and Fukuyama-san from Class A — and Himeno-san from Ichinose's class — with half a minute of conversation each; she forms the volleyball team almost single-handedly; “That first year, Amasawa-san. I owe her a debt.” In the final, she's the anchor — she throws herself in front of Himeno-san's frozen pass — controls the rally — saves the match — she slides to receive Ibuki-san's toss pass — her desperate volleyball saves — the 7–6 lead is hers; Horikita gives her everything until she's spent; the final's final point lands out of bounds; the winners are overwhelmed by the despair of winning.

- **Spoken · Kushida Kikyō** (p.274) — “Horikita… san? … Oh, you’re here.”

- **Spoken · Kushida Kikyō** (p.275) — “That first year, Amasawa-san. I owe her a debt.”
- **Spoken · Kushida Kikyō** (p.275) — “I don’t want to explain my reasons, but to repay that debt, I’m fine with helping you.”
- **Spoken · Kushida Kikyō** (p.277) — “Do you have any conditions for the remaining three? You want to avoid anybody from Ryuuen-kun’s class, is that all?”
- **Spoken · Kushida Kikyō** (p.277) — “Got it. Then, just wait for a bit.”
- **Spoken · Kushida Kikyō** (p.278) — “They say they’ll take part in volleyball. Himeno-san isn’t the best at it, but she agreed to do it if the five of us backed her up. You can leave the competition to us, so it’ll be alright.”

- **Caveat** (p.282) — Himeno-san froze; Kushida-san forced her way in front of her to try to receive the ball, but she could not control its power — narration, not speech.
- **Trait** (p.273–285) — New facade, same pragmatism — she pays her debts; she builds teams in seconds; she rides the fear of losing Class A to last place; she saves the final point with a slide receive; "enemy of my enemy is my friend."

I don't want her to come back to haunt me.” — Kushida's personality underwent a profound change; “it's heading in the direction of growth, I have to believe.”

#### p.84–86 — Kushida's silence; Satō's failed appeal

The day before the festival; Kushida hardly interfered with any of the festival's activities in the weeks leading up; she's been going home immediately after school without helping out; she visits Horikita several times to come up with ideas (some have been adopted, though minor) — but she did not participate in any practice sessions to make sure they were all on the same page. Satō called out to Kushida as she got up — Kushida stopped and turned around on the spot.

- **Spoken · Satō Maya** (p.85) — “I'd like to do some final checks for tomorrow's event and also practice my activities for the day. Do you think you'll have time today?” — Satō, trying as much as possible not to let her guard down.
- **Spoken** (p.85) — “I'm sorry, Satō-san. I have something I really can't miss after school.” — Kushida; this was not the first time.
- **Spoken · Satō Maya** (p.85) — “Listen, you keep refusing me like that. When are you seriously going to cooperate?” — Satō.
- **Spoken** (p.85) — “Then why don't you remove me? I don't think there are any other decent candidates.” — a merciless remark, but a fair one; a student who isn't playing the maid role wouldn't be a substitute.
- **Spoken** (p.85) — “I'll see you tomorrow at the festival. Bye.” — though her tone of voice was the same as the gentle Kushida, her actions could be taken as cold; she refused Satō's proposal until the end and left the classroom.
- **Caveat** (p.85) — “Did she simply not want to spend time with classmates who know her true nature? Or was it really that she had something she can't miss? Obviously, the atmosphere in the classroom deteriorated, but that can't be helped.” — the narration.

Besides, Kushida-senpai herself is beginning to think that it's okay to throw herself into this environment. So, she wants to end her backwards relationship with me and look forward.” — Yagami.
